# Extraction ledger: openproposal, lock with-open-prs

One row per IR element: the element's own bytes, the fact the mapping took from them, and what produced that fact.
Derived from the committed IR, the mapping files and the records — regenerate with `npm run ledger`; never edit by hand.

1762 elements: 1463 claimed by a rule, 276 by a record, 23 claimed by nothing; 0 claimed on a digest the element has moved off.

| `appendix-b:immutable` | B | `immutable` \| 10 \| Set once at creation. Neither party may change it. | rendered on the site | rule | `appendix-row` |
| `appendix-b:lifecycle` | B | `lifecycle` \| 2 \| Live state of the object. The party its section names moves it between the stated values. Not a term. | rendered on the site | rule | `appendix-row` |
| `appendix-b:mixed` | B | `mixed` \| 5 \| The keys of the object carry different markers: in the Mutability column of its key table, in its lead-in for composed keys, or on its rows for `pricing[]`. | rendered on the site | rule | `appendix-row` |
| `appendix-b:recorded` | B | `recorded` \| 4 \| Written by the party that acted. Append-only: an entry is never edited or removed. Not a term. | rendered on the site | rule | `appendix-row` |
| `appendix-b:requestable` | B | `requestable` \| 6 \| The buyer MAY ask for a value outside the declared space. An ask changes the value only through a seller `grant` or `counter` (§ 4.3.1). A change on the seller's own initiative follows § 4.1.3. | rendered on the site | rule | `appendix-row` |
| `appendix-b:selectable` | B | `selectable` \| 16 \| The buyer chooses from a seller-declared set. Each option MAY carry a price delta. Resolves without a round trip. | rendered on the site | rule | `appendix-row` |
| `appendix-b:seller-set` | B | `seller-set` \| 45 \| The seller sets it and MAY revise it while the `status` of the object that carries it is `draft`, `published` or `under_review`. § 4.3.2 states what a revision does to a line item `under_review`. The buyer cannot change it directly. | rendered on the site | rule | `appendix-row` |
| `appendix-b:settable` | B | `settable` \| 4 \| The buyer supplies a value within seller-declared bounds. Resolves without a round trip. | rendered on the site | rule | `appendix-row` |
| `appendix-b:supplied` | B | `supplied` \| 1 \| The buyer provides data or an artifact. The seller does not pre-populate it. | rendered on the site | rule | `appendix-row` |

| `code:4.1.1:1` | 4.1.1 | $.version $.status $.assent $.negotiation_history $.change_requests $.best_for $.not_suitable_for $.seasonality_notes $.line_items[*].status $.line_items[*].availability $.line_items[*].addressable_scale $.line_items[*].assets $.line_items[*].buyer_supplied.expected_match_rate $.line_items[*].buyer_supplied.segments $.line_items[*].buyer_supplied.suppression_list $.line_items[*].pricing[*].min_rate $.line_items[*].hold $.line_items[*].commitment.derived_units $.line_items[*].external_references | informative | decision | D-165: § 4.1.1's fenced block lists the twenty paths a binding record removes. A block of paths is how this document writes a profile's member list, and this one states what is taken out rather than what is kept. What does the block state? → chose `the-operand-list-of-a-transformation` |
| `code:5.5:1` | 5.5 | accepted_exclusions: types: domains:    { enforcement: pre_serve, max_entries: 5000 } apps:       { enforcement: pre_serve } categories: { enforcement: pre_serve } keywords:   { enforcement: best_effort, max_entries: 500 } verification: ["integralads.com", "doubleverify.com"] exclusions: domains:    ["example-gambling.example"] apps:       [{ store: roku_channel_store, bundle: "123456" }] categories: [{ taxonomy: 7, ids: ["181"] }]         # IAB Content Taxonomy 3.0: Casinos & Gambling keywords:   { url: "https://agency-a.example/lists/keywords-2026-10.json",   # a long list, by reference digest: "sha256:ca833e169e39bcaae9fc5b74f8dd3cfddf5b1c4158b6bc19bb3ae9112a347b06" } | an example payload of `LineItem` | rule | `yaml-example-block` |
| `code:5.5:2` | 5.5 | geo: allowed: ["US"] default: { include: ["US"] } value:   { include: ["US"], exclude: ["US-TX"] }   # the buyer's write | an example payload of `LineItem` | rule | `yaml-example-block` |
| `code:5.5:3` | 5.5 | markets: available:  ["SG", "TH", "ID", "MY"] included:   ["SG", "TH"] deltas:     { "ID": { delta_cpm: 18.00 }, "MY": { delta_cpm: 21.00 } } max_select: 4 selected:   ["SG", "ID"]   # the buyer's write: runs in these two, and pays the ID delta | an example payload of `LineItem` | rule | `yaml-example-block` |
| `code:5.5:4` | 5.5 | # Late fringe, 23:00 to 01:00, Monday night into Tuesday daypart: allowed: - { days: [monday],  start: "23:00", before: "24:00" } - { days: [tuesday], start: "00:00", before: "01:00" } default: include: - { days: [monday],  start: "23:00", before: "24:00" } - { days: [tuesday], start: "00:00", before: "01:00" } time_zone: "America/New_York" | an example payload of `LineItem` | rule | `yaml-example-block` |
| `code:5.6:1` | 5.6 | agreed_rate = (list_rate + sum of delta_cpm) × product of (1 + delta_fraction) | — | **nothing claims it** | — |
| `concept:1.1:1` | 1.1 | `id` \| `Deal.id`, `Bid.dealid` \| None: the ID in `deal_origin`'s namespace. The `external_references[]` entry with `reference_type: deal` records it (§ 5.10) | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:1.1:10` | 1.1 | `terms.countries` \| The countries of `geo` or `markets` in effect, in alpha-3 (below), without a country excluded whole. Unset when the line item declares neither | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:1.1:11` | 1.1 | `terms.dealfloor` \| `Deal.bidfloor` \| `agreed_rate` of the committed `pricing[]` entry | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:1.1:12` | 1.1 | `terms.cur` \| `Deal.bidfloorcur` \| That entry's `currency`, always sent | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:1.1:13` | 1.1 | `terms.pricetype` \| `Deal.at`, the same value, omitted for Dynamic (0) \| That entry's `price_type`, always sent | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:1.1:14` | 1.1 | `terms.guar` \| `Deal.guar` \| `1` when the mechanism in effect is `programmatic_guaranteed`, `0` otherwise | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:1.1:15` | 1.1 | `terms.units` \| `commitment.amount` when `basis` is `units`, otherwise `derived_units` (§ 5.6.5) | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:1.1:16` | 1.1 | `terms.totalcost` \| The seller total (§ 5.6.5) | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:1.1:17` | 1.1 | `ext.baseline` \| The agreed baseline `VersionRef` (§ 2.2), with `seller_domain`, so a DSP can join the deal to its record. OPTIONAL. The Deals API 1.0 doesn't define this key | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:1.1:18` | 1.1 | `Order.currency` \| The committed `pricing[]` entry's `currency` | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:1.1:19` | 1.1 | `Line.startdate`, `Line.enddate` \| `commitment.flight.start`, `commitment.flight.end` | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:1.1:2` | 1.1 | `origin` \| `deal_origin` | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:1.1:20` | 1.1 | `Line.ratetype` \| That entry's `cost_method`: `CPM`, `CPMV`, and `CPC` as written, `FLAT` as `FlatRate`, and `TIME` with `rate_unit: day` as `CPD` | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:1.1:21` | 1.1 | `Line.rate` \| That entry's `agreed_rate` | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:1.1:22` | 1.1 | `Line.qty` \| `commitment.amount` when `basis` is `units`, `1` for `FLAT`, otherwise `derived_units` (§ 5.6.5) | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:1.1:23` | 1.1 | `Line.cost` \| The seller total (§ 5.6.5) | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:1.1:24` | 1.1 | `Line.frequencycount`, `Line.frequencyinterval` \| `frequency_cap.count` in effect, and `frequency_cap.period` in OpenDirect's spelling, with `flight` as `LineDuration` | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:1.1:3` | 1.1 | `seller` \| The proposal's `seller.domain` | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:1.1:4` | 1.1 | `wseat` \| `Deal.wseat` \| `buyer_requirements.seats.ids[]` | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:1.1:5` | 1.1 | `adtypes` \| From `channel`: `display` 1, `video` 2, `audio` 3, `native` 4, and `ctv` 2 when `placement.plcmt` is present. Otherwise unset: the record doesn't state the ad's type | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:1.1:6` | 1.1 | `pubcount` \| On a `dooh` line item, `1` when the properties in effect belong to one inventory owner and `2` when they belong to more. `domains[]` names each owner (§ 5.2.4). Otherwise unset | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:1.1:7` | 1.1 | `dinventory` \| On a `dooh` line item, `1` when at least one property is in effect and each property in effect lists its `screens[]`. Otherwise unset | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:1.1:8` | 1.1 | `inventory.inclinventory` \| `[3]` when `dinventory` is `1`. Otherwise unset | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:1.1:9` | 1.1 | `terms.startdate`, `terms.enddate` \| `commitment.flight.start`, `commitment.flight.end` | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:1.2:1` | 1.2 | Message authentication \| Which origin sent this request or response? \| An HTTP Message Signature, with the key in that origin's Web Bot Auth directory \| This specification's Web Bot Auth profile (§ 2.8) \| Nothing. The object never carries or re-proves it | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:1.2:2` | 1.2 | Authorization \| What may this party, or its agent, do? \| The seller's eligibility rules; a mandate the principal publishes or signs \| This specification for the rules (§ 5.8); the AAMP authority document (provisional name) for mandates \| The rules only, never the proof | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:1.2:3` | 1.2 | Legal assent \| Which legal entity agreed to which bytes? \| Two matching `assent[]` entries over one `record_digest`, with both parties named in the record; as optional evidence, the signed messages that carried them \| This specification for the record, digest, entry, and what each party keeps (§ 4.1.1, § 4.1.2); § 2.8 for how a message is signed and its key found \| Parties, digest, entries | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:2.8.2:1` | 2.8.2 | Tag \| `tag="web-bot-auth"` \| `tag="web-bot-auth"` | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:2.8.2:2` | 2.8.2 | Count \| Exactly one `web-bot-auth` member in `Signature-Input`, and exactly one `Signature-Agent` member, keyed to its label. A verifier doesn't verify members with other tags, but every member must be well formed (RFC 9421 §§ 4.1–4.2) \| Same | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:2.8.2:3` | 2.8.2 | Components \| `@method`, `@target-uri`, `@authority`, `"signature-agent";key=<label>` \| `@status`, `"@method";req`, `"@target-uri";req`, `"signature-agent";key=<label>`, and, when the request carried a `web-bot-auth` signature, `"signature";req;key=<request label>` | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:2.8.2:4` | 2.8.2 | Body \| With a body, also `content-type` and `content-digest`. `Content-Digest` (RFC 9530) uses `sha-256`, and a verifier checks it against the body bytes \| Same | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:2.8.2:5` | 2.8.2 | Parameters \| `created`; `expires`, at most 300 seconds after `created`; `keyid`, the key's thumbprint; `alg="ed25519"`; and `nonce`, 64 random bytes as unpadded base64url or padded base64 \| The same, without `nonce` | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:2.8.2:6` | 2.8.2 | Clock \| A verifier allows 60 seconds of clock skew \| Same | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:2.8.6:1` | 2.8.6 | `400` \| The signature fields don't parse \| Section 5.4 | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:2.8.6:2` | 2.8.6 | `403` \| The signature fails verification or a rule of this profile, or a party that requires a signature gets none \| Section 5.3 | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:2.8.6:3` | 2.8.6 | `429` \| The `nonce` was used before. Sign again with a new one \| Section 5.3; RFC 9421 § 7.2.2 | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:2.8.6:4` | 2.8.6 | `503` \| The verifier can't check and record the `nonce`. Retry later, as `Retry-After` says, with the same or a new signature \| Appendix C.6 | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:4.3.1:1` | 4.3.1 | `publish` \| seller \| version 1 of a new or `draft` proposal, or the next `version` for a change to offered terms (§ 4.1.3); `published`. Moving a `draft` line item in a frozen version: neither (§ 4.3.2) | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:4.3.1:10` | 4.3.1 | hold request, grant, decline, release, re-offer \| buyer; seller; seller; buyer, or seller on a revision (rule 2); seller on a revision (rule 2) \| `hold` only; no entry | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:4.3.1:2` | 4.3.1 | availability read \| buyer \| an `AvailabilityRead` (§ 3.3), answered with the figures § 3.3 names, with no change of state; no entry | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:4.3.1:3` | 4.3.1 | `request` \| buyer \| asks a `requestable` value on one line item against a named `version`, stated as a `HistoryPath` (§ 4.1) with `requested_value`; `requested` | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:4.3.1:4` | 4.3.1 | `grant` \| seller \| of a `request`: new `version` with the requested value in force as an offered term; `granted` | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:4.3.1:5` | 4.3.1 | `counter` \| seller \| of a `request`: new `version` with another value; `countered`. A `counter` of `pricing[].list_rate` MAY also change `included[]`, with `paths[]` naming both | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:4.3.1:6` | 4.3.1 | `decline` \| seller \| no new `version`. Of a `request`: `declined`. Of a `commit`: the seller's `assent[]` entry; no entry | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:4.3.1:7` | 4.3.1 | `commit` \| buyer \| a `Commit` (§ 5.6.1): the buyer's `assent[]` entry, which pins the `version`, and its writes (§ 3.4); no entry | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:4.3.1:8` | 4.3.1 | `accept` \| seller \| of a `commit`: the seller's `assent[]` entry, no new `version`; no entry | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:4.3.1:9` | 4.3.1 | `withdraw` \| seller: line item or proposal; buyer: its own ask \| `withdrawn`. A seller's is one entry. For each line item it moved to `withdrawn`, its `paths[]` holds a `HistoryPath` with that `line_item_id` and the `pointer` `/status`. Of the buyer's `commit`: the buyer's `assent[]` entry; no entry | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:4.3.4:1` | 4.3.4 | `offer_window.end` \| the offer window \| every `published`, `under_review`, or `sold_out` line item is `expired`; open asks are void | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:4.3.4:2` | 4.3.4 | `pricing[].price_valid_until` \| the firm entry \| `status` unchanged; a later `commit` under that entry is rejected (§ 5.6.1), and the seller SHOULD `publish` or `withdraw` | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `concept:4.3.4:3` | 4.3.4 | `hold.valid_until` \| the hold \| `hold.status` becomes `expired`; `status` unchanged | informative | rule | `concept-row` |
| `def:1.1:transaction_mechanism.direct_io` | 1.1 | `direct_io` \| OpenDirect 2.1 · Order + Line \| `order`, `line` \| An OpenDirect 2.1 ChangeRequest on the booked Line (`lineid`). `change_requests[]` (§ 4.4) records it against the baseline `VersionRef` (§ 2.2). A partial cancel uses ChangeRequestLine, and a full cancel uses Line `?cancel` at the effective date | a term of `TransactionMechanism` | rule | `header-named-value-row` |
| `def:1.1:transaction_mechanism.preferred_deal` | 1.1 | `preferred_deal` \| Deals API 1.0 · `Deal` + OpenRTB 2.6 · `Deal` \| `deal` \| By agreement between implementers, against the same baseline. A full stop is `sellerstatus` 4 (archived) | a term of `TransactionMechanism` | rule | `header-named-value-row` |
| `def:1.1:transaction_mechanism.private_auction` | 1.1 | `private_auction` \| Deals API 1.0 · `Deal` + OpenRTB 2.6 · `Deal` \| `deal` \| As for `preferred_deal` | a term of `TransactionMechanism` | rule | `header-named-value-row` |
| `def:1.1:transaction_mechanism.programmatic_guaranteed` | 1.1 | `programmatic_guaranteed` \| OpenDirect 2.1 · Order + Line, as the reservation; Deals API 1.0 · `Deal` \| `order`, `line`, `deal` \| The reservation: as for `direct_io`. The deal: as for `preferred_deal` | a term of `TransactionMechanism` | rule | `header-named-value-row` |
| `def:2.1:LineItemSummary` | 2.1 | `LineItemSummary` \| lists these paths of a line item (§ 5): | informative | rule | `lead-in-restates` |
| `def:2.1:PackageSummary` | 2.1 | `PackageSummary` \| keys: | the same as `PackageSummary` | rule | `lead-in-restates` |
| `def:2.1:ProposalSummary` | 2.1 | `ProposalSummary` \| adds this key: | informative | rule | `lead-in-restates` |
| `def:2.2:Amount` | 2.2 | `Amount` \| Number within the limits of § 4.1.1 \| Money. It carries no currency: § 5.6 states which one it reads in | informative | rule | `shared-type-restates` |
| `def:2.2:ApprovalStatus` | 2.2 | `ApprovalStatus` \| `PENDING` \| `APPROVED` \| `REJECTED` \| An approval outcome, in the spelling of OpenDirect 2.1's ChangeRequest `status` | the same as `ApprovalStatus` | rule | `shared-type-restates` |
| `def:2.2:ApprovalStatus#form/values:1` | 2.2 | `PENDING` \| `APPROVED` \| `REJECTED` | the same as `ApprovalStatus` | rule | `restated-value-list` |
| `def:2.2:CalendarUnit` | 2.2 | `CalendarUnit` \| `hour` \| `day` \| `week` \| `month` \| A calendar bucket | the same as `CalendarUnit` | rule | `shared-type-restates` |
| `def:2.2:CalendarUnit#form/values:1` | 2.2 | `hour` \| `day` \| `week` \| `month` | the same as `CalendarUnit` | rule | `restated-value-list` |
| `def:2.2:Digest` | 2.2 | `Digest` \| String of 71 characters: `sha256:` followed by the 64 lowercase hexadecimal characters of the SHA-256 of its input \| The value carries the algorithm, and a field name never names it. `sha256` is the only algorithm in this version. Each field that holds a `Digest` states its input | informative | rule | `shared-type-restates` |
| `def:2.2:DocumentRef` | 2.2 | `DocumentRef` \| pins a binding document. The object is open, so its publisher can add a version or a date. It is never inlined. The publisher keeps every version retrievable, and the counterparty stores a copy. | the same as `DocumentRef` | rule | `lead-in-restates` |
| `def:2.2:Domain` | 2.2 | `Domain` \| String: lowercase ASCII, internationalized labels in A-label form (RFC 5890), with no scheme, port, path, or trailing dot \| Producers MUST write this form. Consumers MUST compare byte for byte and MUST NOT normalize a value before comparing it. A value read from ads.txt or sellers.json is converted to this form before comparison | informative | rule | `shared-type-restates` |
| `def:2.2:Duration` | 2.2 | `Duration` \| String: the ISO 8601 grammar of RFC 3339 Appendix A without its week form (`P14D`, not `P2W`) \| A length of time. A comparison names its anchor. A length within the playback of an ad or a placement, such as a play, view, read, loop, slot, or refresh, is instead an Integer whose key ends `_s` or `_ms`, except a key imported from another standard, which keeps its spelling (§ 2.6) | informative | rule | `shared-type-restates` |
| `def:2.2:EntryRef` | 2.2 | `EntryRef` \| String: `<domain>/<collection>/<entry_id>@<version>`, with no `/` or `@` inside a part \| A named entry at one exact version, whose content does not change. `<domain>` is a `Domain`. It carries no digest | informative | rule | `shared-type-restates` |
| `def:2.2:Ext` | 2.2 | `Ext` \| `Map[Domain, Object]` \| The extension container. § 2.5 states its rules | informative | rule | `shared-type-restates` |
| `def:2.2:FieldPointer` | 2.2 | `FieldPointer` \| String: a JSON Pointer (RFC 6901) \| A location in the object this specification defines | informative | rule | `shared-type-restates` |
| `def:2.2:Fraction` | 2.2 | `Fraction` \| Number from 0 to 1 \| A proportion | informative | rule | `shared-type-restates` |
| `def:2.2:FractionDelta` | 2.2 | `FractionDelta` \| Number greater than -1 \| Used only by `delta_fraction` (§ 3.1) | informative | rule | `shared-type-restates` |
| `def:2.2:HttpsUrl` | 2.2 | `HttpsUrl` \| A `Url` whose scheme is `https` \| A location fetched over TLS | informative | rule | `shared-type-restates` |
| `def:2.2:IanaZone` | 2.2 | `IanaZone` \| String: a name in the IANA time zone database, such as `America/New_York` or `UTC` \| A time zone | informative | rule | `shared-type-restates` |
| `def:2.2:Instant` | 2.2 | `Instant` \| String: an RFC 3339 `date-time` in whole seconds, ending in `Z` \| A point in time. One written form, so one instant has one digest input | informative | rule | `shared-type-restates` |
| `def:2.2:IntegerFlag` | 2.2 | `IntegerFlag` \| Integer: `0` (no) \| `1` (yes) \| A yes or no that another standard writes as an integer. Each field says what `1` affirms | informative | rule | `shared-type-restates` |
| `def:2.2:IntegerFlag#form/values:1` | 2.2 | `0` (no) \| `1` (yes) | stated in the schema of `IntegerFlag` | rule | `restated-value-list` |
| `def:2.2:Interval` | 2.2 | `Interval` \| is an absolute window, with `end` later than `start`. | the same as `Interval` | rule | `lead-in-restates` |
| `def:2.2:LineItemStatus` | 2.2 | `LineItemStatus` \| A `ProposalStatus`, or `sold_out` \| A line item's `status`, moved as § 4.3.2 states | the same as `LineItemStatus` | rule | `shared-type-restates` |
| `def:2.2:Measurer` | 2.2 | `Measurer` \| A `Side`, or a `RegistrableDomain` \| Who counts or measures. A seller writes `seller`, never its own domain. Two values name one party only when they are equal byte for byte. A `qualifier` beside a value names a product or method of that party and is not compared | informative | rule | `shared-type-restates` |
| `def:2.2:Party` | 2.2 | `Party` \| names a legal party. `domain` is the identifier, and `registry_id` is an attestation. An absent or unresolvable `registry_id` MUST NOT make a document malformed, and a consumer MUST ignore a resolved entry whose verified domain is not `domain`. The Agent Registry defines its reference format and how it exposes the verified domain. | the same as `Party` | rule | `lead-in-restates` |
| `def:2.2:ProposalStatus` | 2.2 | `ProposalStatus` \| `draft` \| `published` \| `under_review` \| `agreed` \| `withdrawn` \| `expired` \| A proposal's `status`, computed by § 4.3.3 | the same as `ProposalStatus` | rule | `shared-type-restates` |
| `def:2.2:ProposalStatus#form/values:1` | 2.2 | `draft` \| `published` \| `under_review` \| `agreed` \| `withdrawn` \| `expired` | the same as `ProposalStatus` | rule | `restated-value-list` |
| `def:2.2:Range` | 2.2 | `Range` \| bounds a value of type `T`, written `Range[T]`. At least one bound is present, and `min` is no greater than `max`. | informative | rule | `lead-in-restates` |
| `def:2.2:RegistrableDomain` | 2.2 | `RegistrableDomain` \| A `Domain` that is the Public Suffix List registrable domain, taken over the whole list, PRIVATE section included \| It names a business | informative | rule | `shared-type-restates` |
| `def:2.2:SeatRef` | 2.2 | `SeatRef` \| names a buyer's seats. A seat always travels with its DSP. | the same as `SeatRef` | rule | `lead-in-restates` |
| `def:2.2:Side` | 2.2 | `Side` \| `buyer` \| `seller` \| It says which side, never who that side is. `Party` says who | the same as `Side` | rule | `shared-type-restates` |
| `def:2.2:Side#form/values:1` | 2.2 | `buyer` \| `seller` | the same as `Side` | rule | `restated-value-list` |
| `def:2.2:TaxonomyCodes` | 2.2 | `TaxonomyCodes` \| holds codes in one taxonomy. Each field that holds it names the list `taxonomy` comes from and whether an id covers its descendants. | informative | rule | `lead-in-restates` |
| `def:2.2:TimeUnit` | 2.2 | `TimeUnit` \| A `CalendarUnit`, or `flight` \| `flight` is the whole of `commitment.flight` | the same as `TimeUnit` | rule | `shared-type-restates` |
| `def:2.2:Url` | 2.2 | `Url` \| String: an absolute `http` or `https` URL (RFC 3986) \| A location a client can fetch | informative | rule | `shared-type-restates` |
| `def:2.2:VersionRef` | 2.2 | `VersionRef` \| locates an offer at a version. It carries no digest. A `proposal_id` is unique only within a `seller.domain` (§ 4.1): a `VersionRef` inside a proposal resolves against that proposal's `seller`, and one outside a proposal carries `seller_domain`. | the same as `VersionRef` | rule | `lead-in-restates` |
| `def:2.2:VocabularyTerm` | 2.2 | `VocabularyTerm` \| String: a bare core term, or `<domain>:<term>` with `<domain>` a `Domain` the definer controls. A term is ASCII letters, digits, `_`, and `-` \| See below | informative | rule | `shared-type-restates` |
| `def:2.2:ZoneName` | 2.2 | `ZoneName` \| An `IanaZone`, or `viewer_local` \| `viewer_local` is where the ad renders. On a `dooh` line item that is each screen's own zone, which its `time_zone` names when present | informative | rule | `shared-type-restates` |
| `def:2.3:HoldLimits` | 2.3 | `HoldLimits` \| keys: | the same as `HoldLimits` | rule | `lead-in-restates` |
| `def:2.3:ManifestEntry` | 2.3 | `ManifestEntry` \| holds the keys of `ProposalSummary` (§ 2.1) and this one: | the same as `ManifestEntry` | rule | `lead-in-restates` |

| `field:4.1:assent[]` | 4.1 | `assent[]` \| Array[AssentEntry] \| recorded \| The parties' acts (§ 4.1.2). Each entry is written whole by the party its `role` implies. Entries MUST NOT be edited or removed | property of Proposal, `{"kind":"apply","constructor":"List","arguments":{"T":{"kind":"named","name":"AssentEntry"}}}`, optional | rule | `named-object-row` |
| `field:4.1:brief_id` | 4.1 | `brief_id` \| String \| immutable \| The buyer's brief this proposal answers | property of Proposal, `ExternalId`, conditional | decision | D-094: Twelve rows state a bare `String` or `Integer`. Which domain concept is each one's? → chose `by-what-the-row-says-the-value-is-for` |
| `field:4.1:buyer` | 4.1 | `buyer` \| Party \| immutable \| The entity that assents, usually the agency. Its presence makes the proposal issued to that buyer (§ 4.3). Whom the spend is for is `commitment.advertisers[]` (§ 5.6.1), and whether the buyer may bind that advertiser is authorization (§ 1.2). `legal_name` is REQUIRED here | property of Proposal, `Party`, conditional | rule | `named-object-row` |
| `field:4.1:ext` | 4.1 | `ext` \| Ext \| seller-set \| See Extensions (§ 2.5) | property of Proposal, `Ext`, optional | rule | `named-type-row` |
| `field:4.1:issued_from` | 4.1 | `issued_from` \| VersionRef \| immutable \| The shared proposal and version an issued proposal was made from (§ 4.3). A pointer: nothing is inherited through it | property of Proposal, `VersionRef`, optional | rule | `named-object-row` |
| `field:4.1:line_items[]` | 4.1 | `line_items[]` \| Array[LineItem] \| seller-set \| The line items (§ 5), in the order the seller wrote them. A proposal holds `line_items[]` or `packages[]`, not both | property of Proposal, `{"kind":"apply","constructor":"List","arguments":{"T":{"kind":"named","name":"LineItem"}}}`, conditional | rule | `named-object-row` |
| `field:4.1:negotiation_history[]` | 4.1 | `negotiation_history[]` \| Array[HistoryEntry] \| recorded \| Ordered, append-only. Entries MUST NOT be edited or removed. A commit and its answers are in `assent[]`, not here, and an entry here voids nothing | property of Proposal, `{"kind":"apply","constructor":"List","arguments":{"T":{"kind":"named","name":"HistoryEntry"}}}`, conditional | rule | `named-object-row` |
| `field:4.1:offer_window` | 4.1 | `offer_window` \| Interval \| seller-set \| The window the offer holds: it does not hold before `start` or after `end`. This is the offer window, not the campaign flight | property of Proposal, `Interval`, optional | rule | `named-object-row` |
| `field:4.1:packages[]` | 4.1 | `packages[]` \| Array[Package] \| seller-set \| The line items in packages (§ 4.1.4), in the order the seller wrote them | property of Proposal, `{"kind":"apply","constructor":"List","arguments":{"T":{"kind":"named","name":"Package"}}}`, conditional | rule | `named-object-row` |
| `field:4.1:proposal_id` | 4.1 | `proposal_id` \| String \| immutable \| Identifies the proposal. Unique within `seller.domain` | property of Proposal, `MintedId`, required | rule | `domain-row` |
| `field:4.1:proposal_template_id` | 4.1 | `proposal_template_id` \| String \| immutable \| Links a proposal to the seller's template. Opaque to buyers; used for the seller's reporting | property of Proposal, `ExternalId`, optional | rule | `domain-row` |
| `field:4.1:seller` | 4.1 | `seller` \| Party \| immutable \| The selling entity. `seller.domain` is the seller's identifier for all purposes, including `assent[]`. `legal_name` is REQUIRED here | property of Proposal, `Party`, required | rule | `named-object-row` |
| `field:4.1:spec_version` | 4.1 | `spec_version` \| String \| seller-set \| The version of this specification the document follows, such as `3.0` | property of Proposal, `ExternalId`, required | decision | D-094: Twelve rows state a bare `String` or `Integer`. Which domain concept is each one's? → chose `by-what-the-row-says-the-value-is-for` |
| `field:4.1:status` | 4.1 | `status` \| ProposalStatus \| derived \| Computed from the line items (§ 4.3.3) | property of Proposal, `ProposalStatus`, required | rule | `named-type-row` |
| `field:4.1:type` | 4.1 | `type` \| Enum \| immutable \| `standard` \| `custom` | property of Proposal, `ProposalType`, required | rule | `enum-row` |
| `field:4.1:type#description/values:1` | 4.1 | `standard` \| `custom` | vocabulary `ProposalType` | decision | D-065: What is each vocabulary called, where the specification names a value list only by the row that holds it? → chose `concept-names` |
| `field:4.1:version` | 4.1 | `version` \| Integer \| derived \| Monotonically increasing revision number, starting at 1, held by the seller. Each version is a snapshot of the proposal's offered terms (§ 4.1.3). A version is frozen at the earliest of: the proposal's `status` leaving `draft`; a `negotiation_history[]` or `assent[]` entry naming it; a counterparty receiving it in any representation (§ 2.1). The offered terms of a frozen version MUST NOT change; a change to them creates version `n+1`. The values § 4.1.3 lists as not offered terms change without a new version | property of Proposal, `Count`, required | rule | `domain-row` |
| `field:4.2:best_for[]` | 4.2 | `best_for[]` \| Array[String] \| seller-set \| Advisory planning hints. Non-normative, and never the basis for a match | property of Proposal, `{"kind":"apply","constructor":"List","arguments":{"T":{"kind":"named","name":"Text"}}}`, optional | rule | `domain-row` |
| `field:4.2:description` | 4.2 | `description` \| String \| seller-set \| Prose, not a name. Where the "why buy" case is made, including which channels a multi-channel proposal spans | property of Proposal, `Text`, required | rule | `domain-row` |
| `field:4.2:media_kit_detail` | 4.2 | `media_kit_detail` \| String \| seller-set \| Detail at media-kit depth | property of Proposal, `Text`, optional | decision | D-094: Twelve rows state a bare `String` or `Integer`. Which domain concept is each one's? → chose `by-what-the-row-says-the-value-is-for` |
| `field:4.2:not_suitable_for[]` | 4.2 | `not_suitable_for[]` \| Array[String] \| seller-set \| Advisory exclusion guidance. Non-normative | property of Proposal, `{"kind":"apply","constructor":"List","arguments":{"T":{"kind":"named","name":"Text"}}}`, optional | rule | `domain-row` |
| `field:4.2:seasonality_notes` | 4.2 | `seasonality_notes` \| String \| seller-set \| When the proposal performs differently | property of Proposal, `Text`, optional | rule | `domain-row` |
| `field:4.4:change_requests[]` | 4.4 | `change_requests[]` \| Array[ChangeRequest] \| recorded \| Ordered post-`agreed` history. Each entry carries the fields of the OpenDirect 2.1 ChangeRequest, except its `webhook`, plus `baseline`, `actor`, `at`, and `cancellation`. Outside the binding record (§ 4.1.1) and the offered terms (§ 4.1.3), like `negotiation_history[]`, so an entry changes neither `record_digest` nor `version` | property of Proposal, `{"kind":"apply","constructor":"List","arguments":{"T":{"kind":"named","name":"ChangeRequest"}}}`, optional | rule | `named-object-row` |
| `field:5.10:agreement` | 5.10 | `agreement` \| Agreement \| seller-set \| The master or annual agreement the line item is priced under. | `field:5.10:billing` | 5.10 | `billing` \| Billing \| seller-set \| Who delivers is not always who invoices | property of LineItem, `Billing`, optional | rule | `named-object-row` |

| `field:5.1:ext` | 5.1 | `ext` \| Ext \| seller-set \| See Extensions (§ 2.5) | property of LineItem, `Ext`, optional | rule | `named-type-row` |

| `marker:3:asserted` | 3 | `asserted` \| The seller states it from data the buyer can't recompute. Not a term, and it changes without a new version. | a term of `Marker` | rule | `marker-term` |
| `marker:3:derived` | 3 | `derived` \| Computed by a rule this specification states. Neither party sets it directly. | a term of `Marker` | rule | `marker-term` |
| `marker:3:immutable` | 3 | `immutable` \| Set once at creation. Neither party may change it. | a term of `Marker` | rule | `marker-term` |
| `marker:3:lifecycle` | 3 | `lifecycle` \| Live state of the object. The party its section names moves it between the stated values. Not a term. | a term of `Marker` | rule | `marker-term` |
| `marker:3:mixed` | 3 | `mixed` \| The keys of the object carry different markers: in the Mutability column of its key table, in its lead-in for composed keys, or on its rows for `pricing[]`. | a term of `Marker` | rule | `marker-term` |
| `marker:3:recorded` | 3 | `recorded` \| Written by the party that acted. Append-only: an entry is never edited or removed. Not a term. | a term of `Marker` | rule | `marker-term` |
| `marker:3:requestable` | 3 | `requestable` \| The buyer MAY ask for a value outside the declared space. An ask changes the value only through a seller `grant` or `counter` (§ 4.3.1). A change on the seller's own initiative follows § 4.1.3. | a term of `Marker` | rule | `marker-term` |
| `marker:3:selectable` | 3 | `selectable` \| The buyer chooses from a seller-declared set. Each option MAY carry a price delta. Resolves without a round trip. | a term of `Marker` | rule | `marker-term` |
| `marker:3:seller-set` | 3 | `seller-set` \| The seller sets it and MAY revise it while the `status` of the object that carries it is `draft`, `published` or `under_review`. § 4.3.2 states what a revision does to a line item `under_review`. The buyer cannot change it directly. | a term of `Marker` | rule | `marker-term` |
| `marker:3:settable` | 3 | `settable` \| The buyer supplies a value within seller-declared bounds. Resolves without a round trip. | a term of `Marker` | rule | `marker-term` |
| `marker:3:supplied` | 3 | `supplied` \| The buyer provides data or an artifact. The seller does not pre-populate it. | a term of `Marker` | rule | `marker-term` |
| `rule:1.1:d539a004` | 1.1 | OPTIONAL. | informative | decision | D-082: What does each of the eighteen normative statements of the overview module carry -- a constraint the schema states, a rule a payload decides, a duty on a party, or nothing a conformance check reads? → chose `by-what-decides-it` |
| `rule:2.1:5072813c` | 2.1 | A manifest entry holds it (§ 2.3), and a response over the message binding (§ 1.2) MAY carry it. | an obligation on the seller | decision | D-087: Of the thirty-one normative statements the eight remaining modules hold, which bind a party and which carry nothing a conformance check reads? → chose `by-what-decides-it` |
| `rule:2.1:568d4699` | 2.1 | A full fetch MUST return it, except for line items and packages the requester may not read (§ 5.8). | an obligation on the seller | decision | D-087: Of the thirty-one normative statements the eight remaining modules hold, which bind a party and which carry nothing a conformance check reads? → chose `by-what-decides-it` |
| `rule:2.1:57273714` | 2.1 | REQUIRED when the full form holds `line_items[]` | stated in the schema of `ProposalSummary` | rule | `presence-statement` |
| `rule:2.1:c0741f43` | 2.1 | REQUIRED when the full form holds `packages[]` | stated in the schema of `ProposalSummary` | rule | `presence-statement` |
| `rule:2.2:1e8acd47` | 2.2 | Consumers MUST compare byte for byte and MUST NOT normalize a value before comparing it. | an obligation on the consumer | decision | D-083: What does each normative statement of the kernel module carry, where section 3's marker table states the same words a marker rule already grades? → chose `by-what-decides-it` |
| `rule:2.2:32c53f84` | 2.2 | Anyone MAY define an extension term. | an obligation on the implementation | decision | D-083: What does each normative statement of the kernel module carry, where section 3's marker table states the same words a marker rule already grades? → chose `by-what-decides-it` |
| `rule:2.2:3d1eff57` | 2.2 | An absent or unresolvable `registry_id` MUST NOT make a document malformed, and a consumer MUST ignore a resolved entry whose verified domain is not `domain`. | an obligation on the consumer | decision | D-083: What does each normative statement of the kernel module carry, where section 3's marker table states the same words a marker rule already grades? → chose `by-what-decides-it` |
| `rule:2.2:9c143862` | 2.2 | A field MAY state another effect. | informative | decision | D-083: What does each normative statement of the kernel module carry, where section 3's marker table states the same words a marker rule already grades? → chose `by-what-decides-it` |
| `rule:2.2:b0cdbdd1` | 2.2 | It MAY need authentication | an obligation on the implementation | decision | D-083: What does each normative statement of the kernel module carry, where section 3's marker table states the same words a marker rule already grades? → chose `by-what-decides-it` |
| `rule:2.2:c3db85f7` | 2.2 | For a `channel` (§ 5.1.1) or `cost_method` (§ 5.6.5) term, these effects are a MUST. | an obligation on the implementation | decision | D-083: What does each normative statement of the kernel module carry, where section 3's marker table states the same words a marker rule already grades? → chose `by-what-decides-it` |
| `rule:2.2:df0340d2` | 2.2 | Producers MUST write this form. | stated in the schema of `Domain` | decision | D-083: What does each normative statement of the kernel module carry, where section 3's marker table states the same words a marker rule already grades? → chose `by-what-decides-it` |
| `rule:2.2:e46f15c4` | 2.2 | REQUIRED when the `VersionRef` is outside its proposal | rule of `VersionRef` | rule | `presence-statement` |
| `rule:2.2:ef4efcb1` | 2.2 | Its row MAY narrow the type for that field, by requiring a key the type leaves OPTIONAL or by limiting the values or count the type allows, and says so. | informative | decision | D-083: What does each normative statement of the kernel module carry, where section 3's marker table states the same words a marker rule already grades? → chose `by-what-decides-it` |

| `rule:front:ceff832d` | front | It is not a ratified IAB Tech Lab standard and MUST NOT be treated as one. | informative | rule | `front-matter` |
| `sentence:1.1:0602a8d2` | 1.1 | Any other `cost_method`, and `TIME` with another `rate_unit`, has no `ratetype` and no mapping here. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:1.1:195050a4` | 1.1 | The system named by `deal_origin` (§ 5.9) pushes the `Deal` to the DSP named by `buyer_requirements.seats.dsp`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:1.1:19a49a6f` | 1.1 | The Deals API 1.0 doesn't say whether `enddate` is inclusive, so this mapping states it. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:1.1:39b422a4` | 1.1 | The record holds no value for `Order.name` and `Line.name`, or for `Order.accountid`, `Order.publisherid`, `Order.advertiserbrandid`, and `Line.productid`, which name the server's own `Account`, `Organization`, `AdvertiserBrand`, and `Product` resources. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:1.1:3e5fab5c` | 1.1 | The list names whole countries, so `US` minus `US-TX` sends `USA`, and `geo` in the agreed record holds the exclusion. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:1.1:50a44ede` | 1.1 | OpenDirect's cap counts a unique user, so it has no key for `frequency_cap.per`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:1.1:538c88cd` | 1.1 | OpenDirect 2.1 has the buyer obtain those ids from the publisher and its server, and no key here says which `Product` a line item books. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:1.1:698aed5b` | 1.1 | The record holds no country for an extension term, so the system that pushes the `Deal` supplies the one the term lies in. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:1.1:72a6d2a7` | 1.1 | After the push, the deal's state is the Deals API `sellerstatus` and, for each seat, `BuyerStatus.status`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:1.1:80383fee` | 1.1 | A `direct_io` line item that books in OpenDirect 2.1, and the reservation of a `programmatic_guaranteed` line item, become a `Line` in an `Order`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:1.1:84b27048` | 1.1 | OpenDirect says who acts: on the seller's OpenDirect server, the buyer adds the `Order` to its `Account`, adds the `Line`, and reserves or books it. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:1.1:a0db28a8` | 1.1 | The line item's `status` ends at `agreed` (§ 5.1). | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:1.1:ab9ea5e7` | 1.1 | `commitment.flight.end` and `terms.enddate` are the flight's last second, inclusive. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:1.1:be04708a` | 1.1 | The ids of the `Order` and the `Line` go in `external_references[]`, and the `Line`'s state is its `bookingstatus`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:1.1:c0d148f6` | 1.1 | A `direct_io` line item under one books in another order system, which its `order` entry names (§ 5.10), and changes there. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |

| `sentence:1.2:98f88619` | 1.2 | It is not normative for replay semantics beyond the rule in § 5.6.1, for any lifecycle after `agreed`, or for how a message states the buyer's `Party`, the form of a brief, or how an issued proposal reaches the buyer. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:1.2:cfd91eea` | 1.2 | It is also normative for the lifecycle of the object to its terminal statuses, and for what a section of this document says a request or response carries, such as `errors[]` (§ 2.4), an `AvailabilityRead` (§ 3.3), and a `Commit` and its `CommitResult` (§ 5.6.1). | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |

| `sentence:2.4:06766992` | 2.4 | Informative: over HTTP, `validation`, `not_offered`, and `unresolved_reference` fit 400 Bad Request; `not_eligible` 403 Forbidden; `not_found` 404 Not Found; `not_committable`, `stale_version`, and `record_mismatch` 409 Conflict; `expired` 410 Gone; `idempotency_conflict` 422 Unprocessable Content. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.4:17d27ddf` | 2.4 | A receiver that does not recognize a code, or a key of the object, acts on `retry`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.4:252a6c4d` | 2.4 | Core codes: `validation` \| `not_found` \| `not_offered` \| `unresolved_reference` \| `not_committable` \| `stale_version` \| `expired` \| `idempotency_conflict` \| `not_eligible` \| `record_mismatch`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.4:3dbcc7cd` | 2.4 | An error with `retry: same` fits 503 Service Unavailable. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |

| `sentence:2.5:64988413` | 2.5 | In an object whose keys this specification lists, a key outside `ext` that it does not list is invalid. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.5:7b67929e` | 2.5 | An object whose content another definition sets, such as a `FormatSpec`'s `spec` (§ 5.4), is unaffected, and so are the open objects `DocumentRef` (§ 2.2), `ErrorObject` (§ 2.4), and `CostParameters` (§ 5.6.5). | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.5:b603a40c` | 2.5 | On a `change_requests[]` entry, it is outside the record, as the entry is (§ 4.4). | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |

| `sentence:2.6:0403e4c8` | 2.6 | Prose names a field by its full table path, such as `properties.available[].apps[].store`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.6:0897aa20` | 2.6 | `Object` holds keys that another definition sets. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.6:1336ad16` | 2.6 | A key from another standard keeps that standard's values, as `IntegerFlag` does. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.6:25be7a32` | 2.6 | A row names one key, and a key that holds an array ends `[]`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.6:31294941` | 2.6 | A segment written `<name>` stands for what the lead-in says. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.6:357883db` | 2.6 | A closed list of integers written in a row has Type `Integer`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.6:3cbc8bd4` | 2.6 | The keys sit flat in `X`, and `X` doesn't repeat them. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.6:3fd263ee` | 2.6 | The key table of a `mixed` field adds Mutability as its last column. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.6:42bbdf8d` | 2.6 | The type of an object option in a `Selectable[T]` ends `Option`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.6:440c7082` | 2.6 | The suffix `_ref` is reserved for `DocumentRef` and `EntryRef`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.6:44d6e818` | 2.6 | A Type cell holds `String`, `Integer`, `Number`, `Boolean`, `Enum`, `Object`, a shared type (§ 2.2), a type its section names, or a named key table. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.6:44e7479b` | 2.6 | `Array[T]`, `Map[K, V]`, `Range[T]`, `Selectable[T]` (§ 3.1), `Settable[T]`, `Narrowing[T]`, and `SettableRange[T]` (§ 3.2) take other types. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.6:4c78d105` | 2.6 | A lead-in may limit its table's keys instead, with `holds exactly one key` or `At least one <noun> is present`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.6:5fb76023` | 2.6 | The first segment of a path is a key of `X`, and later segments nest as they do in the object `X` is a subset of. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.6:6cf7220f` | 2.6 | A key table led in by "`X` adds this key" or "`X` adds these keys" follows that block and gives `X` those keys beside the listed paths. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.6:8e67c9dd` | 2.6 | A Form that adds a value to a type reads `A `T`, or `b` `, and one that adds a second type reads `A `T`, or a `U` `. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.6:94459332` | 2.6 | A list built on another standard's list follows its case and names it: `cost_method` is upper case, like OpenDirect 2.1 `Line.ratetype`, and § 1.1 maps it to that list. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.6:99abd19a` | 2.6 | A `VocabularyTerm` field says so and lists its core terms, or points to other lists, whole or in part, and adds any others; every other list is closed, and in a row it opens the Meaning cell. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.6:a174d0b2` | 2.6 | `none` is a value only where it has its own meaning or the key is required. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.6:b3e1306d` | 2.6 | A lead-in that adds "each `<marker>`" gives the composed keys that marker. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.6:be6f6cf1` | 2.6 | An array named by a noun is plural, except the mass nouns `assent[]`, `pricing[]`, `evidence[]`, `verification[]`, and `negotiation_history[]`, and a name another standard spells, such as `playmethod[]`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.6:c1e43a8d` | 2.6 | A Boolean flag may end `_allowed` or `_required`, and a list never does. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.6:c38945dc` | 2.6 | A value that points into a document is a `FieldPointer`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.6:d59b9029` | 2.6 | A lead-in that reads "`X` holds the keys of `Y`" gives `X` every key of `Y`, with the rules `Y` states on them, and the rows of its own table. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.6:d665f5fa` | 2.6 | A list is written once, as ``a` \\| `b``, or ``a`` alone when it has one value, or as the first column of a value table the text names as the list. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.6:d7d7d5d5` | 2.6 | A list taken from another standard keeps that standard's spelling and names it, such as `ApprovalStatus`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.6:d95e1c21` | 2.6 | A lead-in that reads "`X` lists these paths" is followed by a `text` block with one path per line. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.6:da67ca31` | 2.6 | `Y` may take a type, such as `Range[T]`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.6:e89f0125` | 2.6 | `T`, `K`, and `V` stand for a type's arguments, and a Type cell inside that type's table may name them. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.6:eed7c16f` | 2.6 | `Enum` is a closed list of strings, written in the row that uses it, and a generic never takes it: a list a generic takes has a name. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.6:f02bc9c8` | 2.6 | A Required cell is `REQUIRED`, `OPTIONAL`, `RECOMMENDED`, or `REQUIRED when <condition>`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.6:f0f5d5dd` | 2.6 | A name that isn't a noun keeps its form, such as `available[]`, `include[]`, `best_for[]`, and `all_of[]`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.7:0451130b` | 2.7 | Anyone who may read a line item reads its `min_rate` (§ 5.8), so on a `public` line item it is public. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.7:0a2ab4b6` | 2.7 | Agents that can spend read this object, and much of it is text the counterparty wrote: `description`, `media_kit_detail`, `best_for[]`, `not_suitable_for[]`, `seasonality_notes`, `approval`, `creative_policy`, `ext` values, a `FormatSpec`'s `spec`, a change request's `comments`, an error's `detail`, and the document behind a `DocumentRef`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.7:0fe01aca` | 2.7 | A client uses `https`, bounds the size of a response and the number of redirects, and refuses an address inside its own network, on every redirect as well. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.7:264120fd` | 2.7 | `pricing[].min_rate` shows a seller's negotiating position. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.7:63ef97de` | 2.7 | A `digest` shows that the bytes are the ones a `DocumentRef` names. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.7:770e660a` | 2.7 | A client compares it before reading the bytes, and `unresolved_reference` (§ 2.4) reports a mismatch. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.7:900a1170` | 2.7 | A document names URLs its author chose: `proposals[].full_url`, a `DocumentRef`'s `url`, an `Asset`'s `url`, a `CreativeDelivery`'s `endpoint`, and an `App`'s `store_url`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.7:a05f4e19` | 2.7 | `exclusions`, `buyer_supplied.segments[]`, and `buyer_supplied.suppression_list` show a buyer's strategy and can hold data about people. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.7:a5a6458b` | 2.7 | A seller that treats its floor as confidential leaves `min_rate` off, or states it on a `named` line item. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.7:ded42f9e` | 2.7 | § 5.5 states the disclosure rule for `exclusions`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.1:131debe6` | 2.8.1 | The origin's key directory is always at `https://<host>/.well-known/http-message-signatures-directory`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.1:180d2b81` | 2.8.1 | It ignores a member that carries parameters, and a request whose covered member it ignores gets `403`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.1:65217794` | 2.8.1 | A verifier selects a key by the pair of origin and `kid`, never by `kid` alone. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.1:7ec86c38` | 2.8.1 | A verifier accepts a member value only when it is exactly that URL for some host, or exactly the origin `https://<host>`, the form of draft -00. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.1:8d85c3e3` | 2.8.1 | It compares the value as a string, without normalizing it, and refuses any other value with `403`, including `http`, any port, an uppercase host, a trailing `/`, another path, a query, a fragment, and userinfo. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.1:ac1cb74a` | 2.8.1 | An identity is an origin, `https://<host>`, and an application names it by its host, such as `buyer.example`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.1:c12e4dd3` | 2.8.1 | The signer sends that directory URL in the `Signature-Agent` member keyed to the signature's label, with no member parameters, and the signature covers the member as `"signature-agent";key=<label>`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.2:0ce0c8db` | 2.8.2 | `@authority` is that URL's authority. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.2:8d453a2c` | 2.8.2 | `@target-uri` is the request URL as it travels on the wire: its WHATWG URL serialization, normalized as RFC 3986 § 6.2.2 describes (lowercase scheme and host, no default port, dot segments removed), without userinfo, a fragment, or an empty query, and with no other character encoded or decoded. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.2:cf6fab93` | 2.8.2 | Nobody can replay it as the answer to another request, so a response carries no `nonce`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.2:fd94415a` | 2.8.2 | A responder's `Signature-Agent` names the origin of the host the request was sent to (§ 2.8.1), whatever the request URL's scheme and port. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.3:0de19395` | 2.8.3 | A signer also sends `alg="ed25519"`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.3:0ea425d8` | 2.8.3 | A verifier checks each signature with the key the directory provides, and rejects a directory in which an Ed25519 key doesn't sign, a signature whose `created` is more than the 60-second skew allowance in the future, or one whose `expires` has passed. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.3:53f69938` | 2.8.3 | Each signing key is an Ed25519 key (`kty` `OKP`, `crv` `Ed25519`), and its `kid` is its JWK thumbprint (RFC 7638; RFC 8037 Appendix A.3). | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.3:838d5bd8` | 2.8.3 | Each signature covers `"@authority";req` and `content-digest`, and carries `created`, `expires`, the key's thumbprint as `keyid`, and `tag="http-message-signatures-directory"`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.3:924ce060` | 2.8.3 | A verifier fetches a directory over HTTPS only, follows no redirects, and asks for no content coding (`Accept-Encoding: identity`). | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.3:a138a82f` | 2.8.3 | Covering the request's authority stops another domain from serving the key set again, so a directory server signs for its own configured authority, never for the `Host` a client sends. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.3:a4687a55` | 2.8.3 | While a cached copy is less than 5 minutes old, a verifier answers an unknown `kid` from it without fetching again, so a client can't force a fetch with random key ids. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.3:a46ece5b` | 2.8.3 | It treats as a failure any status other than `200`, another media type, a `Content-Encoding`, a body that isn't UTF-8 JSON, and a `Content-Digest` without `sha-256`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.3:b05b889a` | 2.8.3 | A party publishes the new key beside the old one and keeps signing with the old key until verifiers' cached copies can refresh (section 5.5.2): at least its directory response's `Cache-Control` `max-age`, since a CDN can serve the old copy that long, plus 5 minutes. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.3:bebe2fc2` | 2.8.3 | After a failed fetch, a verifier doesn't fetch that origin's directory again for 60 seconds, and with no cached copy it answers `403` meanwhile (Appendix C.5). | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.3:c4e9602c` | 2.8.3 | A fetched directory verifies until the earliest `expires` among its signatures, with no skew allowance. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.3:ee683b3a` | 2.8.3 | A verifier accepts a signature without `alg`, as in Appendix E.2.3, and refuses any other value. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.3:f9d8c7c5` | 2.8.3 | A key directory is a JWK Set served with the media type `application/http-message-signatures-directory+json` (section 5.5.1). | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.4:345665bb` | 2.8.4 | Resolve `@authority` and `@target-uri` from the verifier's own configured authority, never from the request's `Host`, so a request signed for another server fails. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.4:353405c3` | 2.8.4 | Verify the signature and `Content-Digest`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.4:4ae468cc` | 2.8.4 | If one fails, answer `403`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.4:4b39a491` | 2.8.4 | Parse `Signature`, `Signature-Input`, and `Signature-Agent` as Structured Field Dictionaries (RFC 9651 § 3.2). | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.4:52dd2fa8` | 2.8.4 | If a check fails, answer `403`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.4:615ad845` | 2.8.4 | If one fails to parse, answer `400`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.4:771241e7` | 2.8.4 | Select the `web-bot-auth` member and the `Signature-Agent` member with its label. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.4:a3f79a4d` | 2.8.4 | If either is missing or repeated, or the signature doesn't cover the member, answer `403`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.4:bc6b56cc` | 2.8.4 | For a request, check the `nonce` (§ 2.8.5). | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.4:bd4a52e3` | 2.8.4 | What that origin may do is authorization (§ 1.2, § 5.8), and § 4.1.2 states when the signature attributes an `assent[]` entry to a party. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.4:ced61d10` | 2.8.4 | If there is none, answer `403`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.4:d0259c60` | 2.8.4 | Resolve the origin's directory (§ 2.8.3), and select the key whose `kid` equals `keyid`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.4:e05ad9a0` | 2.8.4 | A verifier applies these steps to a message that carries `Signature-Input`: | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.4:e1613972` | 2.8.4 | A message without `Signature-Input` is unsigned, whatever other signature fields it carries. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.4:e341d14c` | 2.8.4 | So does a `Signature-Input` member that isn't an Inner List, a `Signature` member that isn't a Byte Sequence, and the legacy bare-String `Signature-Agent` of section 5.2.1, which this profile doesn't accept. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.5:0f754399` | 2.8.5 | It keeps each `nonce` for the signature's remaining lifetime plus the 60-second skew allowance. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.5:8963e64c` | 2.8.5 | A verifier that can't check and record the `nonce`, because its store is full or unavailable, answers `503` rather than accept the request (Appendix C.6). | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.5:991bcaae` | 2.8.5 | `idempotency_key` keeps its role: a repeated `commit` gets the stored result (§ 5.6.1), and the signature covers it through `content-digest`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8.6:679a5c7a` | 2.8.6 | These statuses answer the transport, before the request is read, so they carry no `errors[]` (§ 2.4). | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2.8:4fffaeb0` | 2.8 | It then refuses an unsigned request with `403` (§ 2.8.6). | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:2:6d07fde0` | 2 | The proposal supplies only identity, `spec_version`, the offer window, `version`, and its packages. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:3.1:0a88d171` | 3.1 | Every field marked `selectable` is a `Selectable[T]`, or a named type that holds its keys, where `T` is the option type. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |

| `sentence:3.1:167f85d2` | 3.1 | `Delta` keys. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |

| `sentence:3.1:4235f893` | 3.1 | With no `selected[]`, `included[]` is in effect. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |

| `sentence:3.2:9509efd3` | 3.2 | `frequency_cap` holds its form in `count`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |

| `sentence:3.2:f5da1fc4` | 3.2 | A field marked `settable` declares its bounds in one of two forms, and the buyer writes one key, `value`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:3.3:27f28f1d` | 3.3 | On a `dooh` line item, they recompute on the screens of the properties in effect, over hours inside both `operating_hours[]` and `daypart`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:3.3:c10596ed` | 3.3 | A buyer reads these figures by sending the seller an `AvailabilityRead`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:3.3:d6de45ae` | 3.3 | The read changes no `status`, writes no history, reserves nothing, and doesn't limit a later decline (§ 5.6.1). | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:3.3:f4451272` | 3.3 | `AvailabilityRead` holds the keys of `BuyerWrites` (§ 3.4) and these: | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:3.4:39df5553` | 3.4 | `BuyerWrites` holds what a buyer writes on one line item. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:3.4:5167ab36` | 3.4 | `BuyerWrites` lists these paths, where `<selectable>` is a field marked `selectable`, and `<settable>` is a field marked `settable` other than `frequency_cap`: | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:3.4:808be197` | 3.4 | It is the buyer's writes on one line item, which a `Commit` (§ 5.6.1) and a hold's `requested_terms` (§ 5.6.2) carry. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:3.4:e06f9ab3` | 3.4 | `LineItemWrites` holds the keys of `BuyerWrites` and this one. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |

| `sentence:3:8bce3258` | 3 | Whose `status`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |

| `sentence:3:acf8d54a` | 3 | A resolved `commitment` is an offer to buy (§ 5.6.1). | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |

| `sentence:3:d1ec9803` | 3 | The proposal's (§ 4.1) for a field defined in § 4; the line item's (§ 5.1) for a field defined in § 5, including `seller-set` sub-fields of a `mixed` field. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |

| `sentence:4.1.1:085d807a` | 4.1.1 | In a proposal with `packages[]`, it leaves each package that holds a line item in S, with only the line items and add-ons in S, and each `$.line_items[*]` path above also selects that path under `$.packages[*].line_items[*]` and `$.packages[*].add_ons[*]`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.1:0d226a75` | 4.1.1 | A non-string mapping key, `.inf`, `.nan`, a recursive alias, an explicit tag, or a second document in the stream has no JSON form and makes the document invalid for this section, and so do duplicate object keys in its JSON form. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.1:219bf348` | 4.1.1 | Every other value is inside the record, named here or not: `proposal_id`, `spec_version`, `seller`, `buyer`, `type`, `proposal_template_id`, `brief_id`, `issued_from`, `offer_window`, `ext`, and each package's `package_id` and `min_commitments[]`; every `seller-set` field, including `hold_terms` and prose (`description`, `media_kit_detail`, `creative_policy`, `format_specs`); the seller's value of a `requestable` field, such as `pricing[].list_rate`, including one a `grant` or `counter` put in force; every option space (`available[]`, `included[]`, `deltas`, `max_select`, `allowed[]`, `min` / `max`, `default`); every `BuyerWrites` value (§ 3.4) and `commitment`, as they stand at `agreed`; `pricing[].agreed_rate`; and every `DocumentRef` (§ 2.2) whole, with any key its publisher added, whose `url` and `digest` pin the bytes. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.1:3f083a18` | 4.1.1 | A `description` that offers an 8.00 `CPM` doesn't change a `list_rate` of 12.00. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.1:4bba336f` | 4.1.1 | Restricting to S leaves `line_items[]` an array of the line items in S, in document order however S is named. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |

| `sentence:4.1.1:62bc0de7` | 4.1.1 | `24.00`, `24`, and the YAML `0x18` are the same value. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.1:642fcd68` | 4.1.1 | The one exception is `buyer_supplied`: if it holds no key after these removals, it is removed too, whether the removals emptied it or it was written empty. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.1:66e6fbb3` | 4.1.1 | Where a value whose Type cell is `String` (§ 2.6) conflicts with a typed field of the same `version` in the binding record, the typed field governs. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.1:67ab8f6f` | 4.1.1 | A `Commit` (§ 5.6.1) carries the buyer's entry and writes, not a copy of the seller's terms. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.1:6c933da9` | 4.1.1 | The requirement on creative (`formats`, `format_specs`, `creative_policy`, `materials_due`) is a term, and so is the permission to deliver a list (`segments_allowed`, `suppression_allowed`, `accepted_exclusions`). | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.1:87d9c76f` | 4.1.1 | Each removed path is one of three things: lifecycle state or sequence position (`version`, `status`, `assent[]`, `negotiation_history[]`, `change_requests[]`, `hold`); a seller assertion, advisory text, an informative figure, or an identifier minted downstream of agreement (`best_for[]`, `not_suitable_for[]`, `seasonality_notes`, `availability`, `addressable_scale`, `expected_match_rate`, `min_rate`, `derived_units`, `external_references[]`); or a delivery (`assets[]`, `segments[]`, `suppression_list`). | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.1:8ca741a7` | 4.1.1 | The binding record is cut from the composed record: the Full Representation of `version` as the seller published it to this buyer, with the buyer's writes (§ 3.4) applied. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.1:9f0d6559` | 4.1.1 | A list the buyer delivers after `agreed` is a delivery and does not change the agreed record (§ 4.1.2). | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.1:9fd49903` | 4.1.1 | It sits only on `assent[]` entries. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.1:a91a7ba7` | 4.1.1 | A value of an `Enum` or of a § 2.2 type, such as an `Instant`, `Duration`, or `VocabularyTerm`, is a typed field. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.1:b10cb27e` | 4.1.1 | A `withdrawal` or `decline` acts on the entries it matches (§ 4.1.2), so its `record_digest` is compared, not recomputed. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.1:b1290f2c` | 4.1.1 | A document that already holds those writes at that `version` is its own composed record. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.1:b86f0ddd` | 4.1.1 | Nothing else narrows: a proposal-level value such as `description` stays as it is. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.1:ea066674` | 4.1.1 | A document carried as YAML is a single YAML document, converted to JSON under the YAML 1.2 core schema: aliases expand, and `<<` is an ordinary key. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.1:ea65ff78` | 4.1.1 | `derived` values. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.1:ee465300` | 4.1.1 | Otherwise, as for a rejected `commit`, a full fetch doesn't show them and they enter no later composed record. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.1:f8e55774` | 4.1.1 | On a line item with a `commitment`, a party sets it to the § 5.6 result before serializing, replacing a written value or adding an absent one. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.2:067e10f7` | 4.1.2 | Each party keeps the agreed record, and a copy of every document a `DocumentRef` in it pins. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.2:110168c4` | 4.1.2 | A line item for which no such pair exists is not agreed, whatever its `status` says. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.2:18100fa4` | 4.1.2 | The entry accepts every `terms_refs[]` entry (§ 5.8) of the line items it names, at the bytes each `digest` pins, so a buyer compares each document with its `digest` first. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.2:248a753f` | 4.1.2 | To check a pair, a party recomputes `record_digest` from the agreed record, not from the current document, and tests that the two entries match. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.2:3720a27b` | 4.1.2 | Each ends the `commitment` entries before it that it matches. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.2:3d67e37e` | 4.1.2 | `role: withdrawal` is written by the buyer, and `role: decline` by the seller. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.2:4185f60b` | 4.1.2 | `assent[]` holds the parties' acts in the order they were written, and only it decides whether a line item is agreed. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.2:42dbc7e5` | 4.1.2 | Adding an `assent[]` entry does not change `version`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |

| `sentence:4.1.2:57dfdeca` | 4.1.2 | Once a matching `acceptance` exists, nothing ends it. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.2:5ca39dff` | 4.1.2 | Once a matching pair exists, an absent or failed signature, or a key later removed from its directory, does not change `agreed`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.2:74cbe1c9` | 4.1.2 | `role: acceptance` is written by the seller, only while a `commitment` entry it matches is live. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.2:7838c492` | 4.1.2 | `AssentEntry` keys: | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |

| `sentence:4.1.2:8e5481e7` | 4.1.2 | No check in this specification requires or verifies a signature, and `agreed` does not depend on one. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.2:aafdf092` | 4.1.2 | `record_digest` covers `seller` and `buyer`, so the signed body also binds the party's name. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.2:addc1297` | 4.1.2 | Requests and responses are both signed, so the seller's `acceptance` and `decline` arrive signed, as the buyer's entries do. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.2:b1e180e2` | 4.1.2 | An `acceptance` that matches no live entry is invalid. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.2:c5c4e5ee` | 4.1.2 | A `commitment` entry is live until the first of these: a matching `withdrawal` or `decline`, its `commit` going stale (§ 4.1.3) or void (§ 4.3.2, § 4.3.4), or `offer_window.end`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |

| `sentence:4.1.2:e95695a5` | 4.1.2 | `role: commitment` is written by the buyer. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.2:ec934f27` | 4.1.2 | The signature attributes the entry to the record's party that `role` implies when it covers the body, through `Content-Digest` (RFC 9530), and the host of the signing origin is that party's `domain` or a subdomain of it, such as `signing.<domain>`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.3:031671ad` | 4.1.3 | `published` for version 1, appended no later than the moment version 1 is frozen, and for a change the seller makes on its own initiative, including a change to `seller-set` prose. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.3:0efead3c` | 4.1.3 | A change to the `line_item_id`s of a package that holds a line item it names also makes it stale. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |

| `sentence:4.1.3:2905c906` | 4.1.3 | `version` is outside the record, so a revision that touches only another line item does not make it stale, and a change to a proposal-level term does. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.3:379f94d5` | 4.1.3 | § 5.6.1 states the test for a `commit`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |

| `sentence:4.1.3:39b87017` | 4.1.3 | Its `action` is one of the following: | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.3:52da3817` | 4.1.3 | The offered terms of a version are every value the seller writes: `immutable` and `seller-set` fields; the seller's value of a `requestable` field, such as `pricing[].list_rate`; the seller-declared structure of `selectable` and `settable` fields (`available[]`, `included[]`, `deltas`, `max_select`, `allowed[]`, `min`, `max`, `default`); and the set of `line_item_id`s the version carries. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.3:7f809fd0` | 4.1.3 | An ask or a hold is on a `version`. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.3:9a678333` | 4.1.3 | `granted` or `countered` for a change that answers a buyer's `requestable` ask: `granted` puts the requested value into force, `countered` puts a different value into force in its place. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.3:ee4166ee` | 4.1.3 | It is stale when the binding record (§ 4.1.1) for the line items it names, with any buyer writes applied, differs between that `version` and the current one. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.3:fdeb7c34` | 4.1.3 | A buyer composes against a named `version` and never increments it. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.4:0ddca2d6` | 4.1.4 | Whatever `basis` the commitment on `line_item_id` uses, a `spend` threshold reads against its seller total (§ 5.6.5), in the currency of the entry committed (§ 5.6), and a `units` threshold reads against the units that entry commits, as `pricing[].unit_bounds` counts them. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.4:31c4d5d7` | 4.1.4 | The holds on the line items in a package's `line_items[]` move as a unit (§ 5.6.2). | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |

| `sentence:4.1.4:4cae9eae` | 4.1.4 | A package can't be bought while a line item in its `line_items[]` is `sold_out` or `withdrawn`, since that line item can't be committed. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
| `sentence:4.1.4:5396c293` | 4.1.4 | A notice on only some of them, or a request whose notices differ in `cancelled_share` or `requested_effective_at`, exercises no option. | informative | rule | `prose-sentence` |
